Hence the sum of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r)$ is the configuration with variables $p, q, r$, ranges $[P, 2P)$, $[Q, 2Q)$ and $[\mu^4, (X/(PQ))^{1/2})$, the constraint $r \prec q$, and the sieve condition of type (S) with $i_{\mathrm{S}}$ the index of $r$. All pieces of the configuration of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r)$, the window pieces included, fall under (P3) by \eqref{eq: P3 criterion}, with $\mathcal{S} = \{p\}$ and $\varpi_{i_0} = r$. Indeed, on every piece, $X^{\sigma} \Le P \Le |p|^2 < 2P$ and $|r|^2 < (X/(PQ))^{1/2}$. The hypothesis $Q \Ge P X^{-2\theta}$ gives $P/Q \Le X^{2\theta}$, and $1 + 2\theta = 2\tau$ by \eqref{eq: parameters}, so
\begin{equation*}
|pr|^2 < 2P \Bigl( \frac{X}{PQ} \Bigr)^{1/2} = 2 \Bigl( \frac{XP}{Q} \Bigr)^{1/2} \Le 2 \bigl( X^{1 + 2\theta} \bigr)^{1/2} = 2X^{\tau}.
\end{equation*}
Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} gives the sums of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, \mu^4)$ and of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r)$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 11 buchstab} asymptotic formulas. By \eqref{eq: lemma 11 buchstab}, the sum of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q)$ over $p \sim P$ and $q \sim Q$ then has an asymptotic formula.
\end{proof}

The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem: three primes} follows that of Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 11}, with $pq$ in place of $p$ as the variable $u$ of the bilinear estimate.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: three primes}
Suppose that $Q \Le 2P$ and $X^{\sigma} \Le PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}$. Let $\mathcal{I}_q \subset [Q, 2Q)$ and $\mathcal{I}_r \subset [X^{\tau}/(PQ), 2Q)$ be intervals, and suppose that $\inf \mathcal{I}_q \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 12 sum}
\sum_{p \sim P,\ |q|^2 \in \mathcal{I}_q,\ q \prec p}\ \sum_{|r|^2 \in \mathcal{I}_r,\ r \prec q} S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)\bigr)
\end{equation}
has an asymptotic formula if one of the following holds:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 12 cases}
\text{(a)}\ PQ \Le X^{\zeta - \varepsilon},\ Q \Le X^{\rho - \varepsilon},\ PQ^2 \Le X^{\nu - \varepsilon}; \qquad \text{(b)}\ P \Le X^{\zeta - \varepsilon},\ Q^2 \Le X^{\rho - \varepsilon},\ PQ^2 \Le X^{\nu - \varepsilon}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Every prime factor of $pqr$ has norm at least $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, since $|p|^2 \Ge |q|^2 \Ge \inf \mathcal{I}_q \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ and $|r|^2 \Ge \inf \mathcal{I}_r \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$. So $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)) = S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, X^{\tau}/(PQ))$. Since $X^{\tau}/(PQ) \Ge X^{\varepsilon} \Ge \mu^4$ by the hypothesis $PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}$, Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} at the level $\mu^4$ gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 12 buchstab}
S'\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)\bigr) = S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, \mu^4) - \sum_{\mu^4 \Le |s|^2 < X^{\tau}/(PQ)} S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s).
\end{equation}
Summed as in \eqref{eq: lemma 12 sum}, the term $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, \mu^4)$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 12 buchstab} is the configuration with variables $p, q, r$, ranges $[P, 2P)$, $\mathcal{I}_q$ and $\mathcal{I}_r$, the constraints $q \prec p$ and $r \prec q$, and the sieve condition of type (C). Summed in the same way, the sum of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s)$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 12 buchstab} is the configuration with the further variable $s$, of range $[\mu^4, X^{\tau}/(PQ))$, the same constraints, and the sieve condition of type (S) with $i_{\mathrm{S}}$ the index of $s$.

For the configuration of type (C), the pieces of hypothesis (a) of Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} have the level $y = \mu^4$ by \eqref{eq: piece level} and fall under (P2), and the straddling pieces fall under (P1). There are no window pieces, since the window \eqref{eq: window} is empty in type (C). For (P1) and (P2), split the indices according to the case of \eqref{eq: lemma 12 cases}:
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
&\text{(a)}\quad \mathcal{U} = \{p, q\},\ \mathcal{V} = \{r\}: && |pq|^2 < 4PQ, \quad |r|^2 < 2Q, \quad |pqr|^2 < 8PQ^2; \\
&\text{(b)}\quad \mathcal{U} = \{p\},\ \mathcal{V} = \{q, r\}: && |p|^2 < 2P, \quad |qr|^2 < 4Q^2, \quad |pqr|^2 < 8PQ^2.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
In both cases, \eqref{eq: lemma 12 cases} bounds the norms of the products over $\mathcal{U}$, over $\mathcal{V}$ and over all indices by $X^{\zeta - \varepsilon}$, $X^{\rho - \varepsilon}$ and $X^{\nu - \varepsilon}$, up to constant factors, as the conditions on the exponents in (P1) and (P2) require.

For the configuration of type (S), all pieces, the window pieces included, fall under (P3) by \eqref{eq: P3 criterion}, with $\mathcal{S} = \{p, q\}$ and $\varpi_{i_0} = s$. Indeed, on every piece,
\begin{equation*}
|pq|^2 \Ge PQ \Ge X^{\sigma}, \qquad |pqs|^2 < 4PQ \cdot \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ} = 4X^{\tau}.
\end{equation*}
Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} gives the configurations of type (C) and of type (S) asymptotic formulas. By \eqref{eq: lemma 12 buchstab}, the sum \eqref{eq: lemma 12 sum} then has an asymptotic formula.
\end{proof}

For the boxes with $PQ \Ge X^{\sigma}$, Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} applies Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} twice at the level $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, as in \eqref{eq: pair identity}. For a prime level $q$, \eqref{eq: buchstab} lowers the level to $w$ only for $w \Le |q|^2$. So Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} can apply \eqref{eq: buchstab} to $S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q)$ at the level $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ only for the pairs with $|q|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$. The pairs with $|q|^2 < X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ form the sum $S_<$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 13 split}. In the sum over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35}, $|p|^2 \Ge P$, $|q|^2 \Ge Q$ and $|r|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, so
\begin{equation*}
|pqr|^2 \Ge PQ \cdot \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ} = X^{\tau}.
\end{equation*}
Hence the norms $|pqr|^2$ exceed the bound $X^{\tau - \varepsilon}$ for $|n|^2$ in Lemma~\ref{lem: level large} and the bound $X^{\sigma}$ in Lemma~\ref{lem: level small}. After Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} lowers the level $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, \cdot)$ to $\mu^4$, the exponents of $p$, $q$ and $r$ also violate the alternative $\sum_{i=1}^{K} x_i \Le \tau - \varepsilon$ of (P1) and (P2). The sum over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35} therefore needs Lemma~\ref{lem: three primes}.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: lemma 13}
Suppose that $X^{\varepsilon} \Le Q \Le 2P$ and $X^{\sigma} \Le PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}$. Split
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 13 split}
\sum_{p \sim P,\, q \sim Q,\, q \prec p} S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q) = S_< + S_\Ge
\end{equation}
according as $|q|^2 < X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ or $|q|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$. Then $S_<$ has an asymptotic formula, and
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: identity 35}
\begin{aligned}
S_\Ge = {} & \sum_{p, q} S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)\bigr) - \sum_{p, q}\ \sum_{\substack{r \prec q \\ |r|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)}} S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)\bigr) \\
& + \sum_{p, q}\ \sum_{\substack{r \prec q \\ |r|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)}}\ \sum_{\substack{s \prec r \\ |s|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)}} S(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where each $\sum_{p, q}$ runs over $p \sim P$, $q \sim Q$, $q \prec p$, $|q|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$. The sum over $(p, q)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35} has an asymptotic formula, and so has the sum over $(p, q, r)$ if (a) or (b) of \eqref{eq: lemma 12 cases} holds.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Every pair $(p, q)$ of the box satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 13 pq}
X^{\sigma} \Le |pq|^2 < 4PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon/2}, \qquad \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ} < \frac{4X^{\tau}}{|pq|^2}.
\end{equation}
The sum $S_<$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 13 split} is the configuration with variables $p, q$, ranges $[P, 2P)$ and $[Q, \min(2Q, X^{\tau}/(PQ)))$, the constraint $q \prec p$, and the sieve condition of type (S) with $i_{\mathrm{S}}$ the index of $q$. By \eqref{eq: piece level}, a piece of hypothesis (a) of Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} has the level $y = \xi_{\lambda_q}$. The range $|q|^2 < X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ of $q$ and \eqref{eq: lemma 13 pq} give, for every pair $(p, q)$ of the piece,
\begin{equation*}
X^{\sigma} \Le |pq|^2 \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon/2}, \qquad y \Le |q|^2 < \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ} < \frac{4X^{\tau}}{|pq|^2},
\end{equation*}
which implies \eqref{eq: P4 large} with $n = pq$. The piece therefore falls under (P4). A straddling piece falls under (P1), since $|pq|^2 < 4PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon/2}$ by \eqref{eq: lemma 13 pq}. By \eqref{eq: window}, the prime $\varpi'$ of a window piece has $|\varpi'|^2 \in J_{\lambda_q}$, so $|\varpi'|^2 < (1 + 1/H)|q|^2$ by \eqref{eq: short intervals}. With $|pq|^2 < 4PQ$ and $|q|^2 < X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, the bound for $|\varpi'|^2$ gives
\begin{equation*}
|pq\varpi'|^2 < 4PQ\, (1 + 1/H)\, |q|^2 < 4(1 + 1/H) X^{\tau} \Le 5X^{\tau}.
\end{equation*}
The window piece falls under (P3) by \eqref{eq: P3 criterion}, with $\mathcal{S} = \{p, q\}$ and $\varpi_{i_0} = \varpi'$, since $|pq|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}$. Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} gives $S_<$ an asymptotic formula.

For $|q|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} at the level $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 13 buchstab q}
S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q) = S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)\bigr) - \sum_{\substack{r \prec q \\ |r|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)}} S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r),
\end{equation}
and Buchstab's identity at the same level, applied to each term $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r)$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 13 buchstab q}, gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 13 buchstab r}
S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r) = S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, X^{\tau}/(PQ)\bigr) - \sum_{\substack{s \prec r \\ |s|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)}} S(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s).
\end{equation}
Inserting \eqref{eq: lemma 13 buchstab r} into \eqref{eq: lemma 13 buchstab q} and summing over the pairs of $S_\Ge$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 13 split} gives \eqref{eq: identity 35}.

The sum over $(p, q)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35} is $\sum_{n \neq 0} a_n S(\mathcal{A}_n, X^{\tau}/(PQ))$, where $a_n$ is the number of pairs $(p, q)$ in the range of $\sum_{p, q}$ with $pq = n$. Each $a_n$ is $0$ or $1$, since $q \prec p$. By \eqref{eq: lemma 13 pq}, every $n$ with $a_n \neq 0$ has $X^{\sigma} \Le |n|^2 \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon/2}$, and the level $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ is less than $4X^{\tau}/|n|^2$. So Lemma~\ref{lem: level large} applies with $C_l = 4$ and $\varepsilon/2$ in place of $\varepsilon$.

The sum over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35} is the sum \eqref{eq: lemma 12 sum} with $\mathcal{I}_q = [\max(Q, X^{\tau}/(PQ)), 2Q)$ and $\mathcal{I}_r = [X^{\tau}/(PQ), 2Q)$, since $r \prec q$ gives $|r|^2 < 2Q$. Then $\inf \mathcal{I}_q \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, and the hypotheses $Q \Le 2P$ and $X^{\sigma} \Le PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem: three primes} are among those of Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13}. Lemma~\ref{lem: three primes} therefore gives the sum over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35} an asymptotic formula if (a) or (b) of \eqref{eq: lemma 12 cases} holds.
\end{proof}

For a box with $PQ < X^{\sigma}$, the level $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ of \eqref{eq: identity 35} exceeds $z(PQ)$, which is, up to a constant factor, the largest constant level that Lemmas~\ref{lem: level large} and~\ref{lem: level small} admit for a sum of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, \cdot)$ over the box. In Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14}, the hypothesis $X^{\sigma - 2\theta} \Le PQ < X^{\sigma}$ and \eqref{eq: j interval} give $j(PQ) \in \{0, 1\}$, and \eqref{eq: z function} gives
\begin{equation*}
z(PQ) = \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+2)}.
\end{equation*}
Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} applies Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} to $S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q)$ at the level $z(PQ)$. This application subtracts the sum of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r)$ over the primes $r \prec q$ with $|r|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$, as in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 buchstab q}. Then \eqref{eq: buchstab} lowers the level of each term $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r)$ of the subtracted sum to $(X^{\tau}/(PQ|r|^2))^{1/(j(PQ)+1)}$. The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} shows in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 three primes} that $j(|pqr|^2) \Le j(PQ) - 1$ for every product $pqr$ of the subtracted sum, and deduces in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 piece level} that the level of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, \cdot)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36} is below $4z(|pqr|^2)$. Under the hypothesis $4\varepsilon < \tau - 3\sigma/2$, the bounds $Q \Le 2P$ and $PQ < X^{\sigma}$ give $|pqr|^2 \Le X^{\tau - 3\varepsilon}$ for every product $pqr$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36}, as \eqref{eq: lemma 14 small products} shows. So the pieces of hypothesis (a) of Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} for the sum over $(p, q, r)$ fall under (P4) of Lemma~\ref{lem: pieces}. The hypothesis $4\varepsilon < \tau - 3\sigma/2$ holds for small $\varepsilon$, since $\tau - 3\sigma/2 = 5\theta/2 - 1/4 > 0$.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: lemma 14}
Suppose that $X^{\varepsilon} \Le Q \Le 2P$ and $X^{\sigma - 2\theta} \Le PQ < X^{\sigma}$, and that $4\varepsilon < \tau - 3\sigma/2$. Split
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 split}
\sum_{p \sim P,\, q \sim Q,\, q \prec p} S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q) = S_< + S_\Ge
\end{equation}
according as $|q|^2 < z(PQ)$ or $|q|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$. Then $S_<$ has an asymptotic formula, and
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: identity 36}
\begin{aligned}
S_\Ge = {} & \sum_{p, q} S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, z(PQ)\bigr) - \sum_{p, q} \sum_{\substack{r \prec q \\ |r|^2 \Ge z(PQ)}} S\Bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, \Bigl(\frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ|r|^2}\Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)}\Bigr) \\
& + \sum_{p, q} \sum_{\substack{r \prec q \\ |r|^2 \Ge z(PQ)}} \sum_{\substack{s \prec r \\ |s|^{2(j(PQ)+1)} \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ|r|^2)}} S(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where each $\sum_{p, q}$ runs over $p \sim P$, $q \sim Q$, $q \prec p$, $|q|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$. The sums over $(p, q)$ and over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36} have asymptotic formulas.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In this proof, a sum or piece sifted at a constant level $y \Ge 1$ has an asymptotic formula, by Lemmas~\ref{lem: level large} and~\ref{lem: level small} with $C_l = 4$ or by (P4) of Lemma~\ref{lem: pieces}, if the product $n$ of the primes in each of its terms satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 criterion}
y < 4z(|n|^2), \qquad |n|^2 \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}.
\end{equation}
For $|n|^2 < X^{\sigma}$, these bounds give \eqref{eq: P4 small} and the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem: level small}. For $|n|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}$, where $z(|n|^2) = X^{\tau}/|n|^2$ by \eqref{eq: j function} and \eqref{eq: z function}, the same bounds give \eqref{eq: P4 large} and the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem: level large}.

By \eqref{eq: j interval}, every pair $(p, q)$ of the box satisfies
\begin{equation*}
X^{\sigma - (j(PQ)+1)\theta} \Le PQ \Le |pq|^2 < 4PQ < 4X^{\sigma - j(PQ)\theta}.
\end{equation*}
If $|pq|^2 < X^{\sigma}$, then the lower bound $X^{\sigma - (j(PQ)+1)\theta} \Le |pq|^2$ and \eqref{eq: j interval} applied to $|pq|^2$ give
\begin{equation*}
X^{\sigma - (j(PQ)+1)\theta} \Le |pq|^2 < X^{\sigma - j(|pq|^2)\theta},
\end{equation*}
so $j(|pq|^2) \Le j(PQ)$. If $j(PQ) = 1$, then $|pq|^2 < 4X^{\sigma - \theta} < X^{\sigma}$, so $|pq|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}$ occurs only for $j(PQ) = 0$. Since $PQ < X^{\sigma}$, also $X^{\tau}/|pq|^2 > X^{\tau}/(4PQ) > 1$ and $16PQ < 16X^{\sigma} \Le X^{\tau}$. With these bounds, \eqref{eq: j function} and \eqref{eq: z function} give
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 pq levels}
\begin{aligned}
z(|pq|^2) &= \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{|pq|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(|pq|^2)+2)} \Ge \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{4PQ} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+2)} > \frac{z(PQ)}{4} && (|pq|^2 < X^{\sigma}), \\
z(PQ) &= \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ} \Bigr)^{1/2} \Le \frac{X^{\tau}}{4PQ} \Le \frac{X^{\tau}}{|pq|^2} = z(|pq|^2) && (|pq|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
By \eqref{eq: lemma 14 pq levels} and the hypothesis $PQ < X^{\sigma}$, every pair $(p, q)$ of the box satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 pq bounds}
z(PQ) < 4z(|pq|^2), \qquad |pq|^2 < 4PQ < 4X^{\sigma} \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}.
\end{equation}

The bounds $Q \Le 2P$ and $PQ < X^{\sigma}$ give
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 pq2}
PQ^2 = PQ \cdot Q \Le PQ\, (2PQ)^{1/2} = \sqrt{2}\, (PQ)^{3/2} < \sqrt{2}\, X^{3\sigma/2}.
\end{equation}
For a prime $\varpi$ with $|\varpi|^2 \Le 4Q$, the bounds $|p|^2 < 2P$ and $|q|^2 < 2Q$, \eqref{eq: lemma 14 pq2} and the hypothesis $4\varepsilon < \tau - 3\sigma/2$ give
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 small products}
|pq\varpi|^2 < 16 PQ^2 < 16\sqrt{2}\, X^{3\sigma/2} \Le X^{\tau - 3\varepsilon} \qquad (|\varpi|^2 \Le 4Q).
\end{equation}

The sum $S_<$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 14 split} is the configuration with variables $p, q$, ranges $[P, 2P)$ and $[Q, \min(2Q, z(PQ)))$, the constraint $q \prec p$, and the sieve condition of type (S) with $i_{\mathrm{S}}$ the index of $q$. By \eqref{eq: piece level}, a piece of hypothesis (a) of Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} has the level $y = \xi_{\lambda_q}$. The range $|q|^2 < z(PQ)$ of $q$ and \eqref{eq: lemma 14 pq bounds} give, for every pair $(p, q)$ of the piece,
\begin{equation*}
y \Le |q|^2 < z(PQ) < 4z(|pq|^2), \qquad |pq|^2 < 4PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}.
\end{equation*}
So a piece of hypothesis (a) satisfies \eqref{eq: lemma 14 criterion} with $n = pq$ and falls under (P4). A straddling piece falls under (P1) by the bound for $|pq|^2$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 pq bounds}. By \eqref{eq: window}, the prime $\varpi'$ of a window piece has $|\varpi'|^2 \in J_{\lambda_q}$, so $|\varpi'|^2 < (1 + 1/H)|q|^2 < 4Q$ by \eqref{eq: short intervals}. The window piece therefore falls under (P1) by \eqref{eq: lemma 14 small products} with $\varpi = \varpi'$. Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} gives $S_<$ an asymptotic formula.

For $|q|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$, Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} at the level $z(PQ)$ gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 buchstab q}
S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q) = S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, z(PQ)\bigr) - \sum_{\substack{r \prec q \\ |r|^2 \Ge z(PQ)}} S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r).
\end{equation}
Every prime $r$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 14 buchstab q} has $|r|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$, so \eqref{eq: z function} gives
\begin{equation*}
|r|^{2(j(PQ)+2)} \Ge z(PQ)^{j(PQ)+2} = \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ}, \qquad \text{that is,} \qquad \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ|r|^2} \Le |r|^{2(j(PQ)+1)}.
\end{equation*}
Hence the level $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$ of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, \cdot)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36} satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 ms}
m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) = \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{PQ|r|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)} \Le |r|^2.
\end{equation}
Since $r \prec q$, every prime $r$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 14 buchstab q} also has $|r|^2 < 2Q$. With \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms}, \eqref{eq: lemma 14 pq2}, the exponent $1/(j(PQ)+1) \Ge 1/2$ and the hypothesis $4\varepsilon < \tau - 3\sigma/2$, the bound $|r|^2 < 2Q$ gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 ms lower}
m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) > \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{2PQ^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)} \Ge \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau - 3\sigma/2}}{2\sqrt{2}} \Bigr)^{1/2} > \frac{X^{(\tau - 3\sigma/2)/2}}{2} \Ge X^{2\varepsilon} \Ge \mu^4.
\end{equation}
By \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms} and \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms lower}, $\mu^4 \Le m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) \Le |r|^2$, so \eqref{eq: buchstab} applies again, at the level $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 buchstab r}
S(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, r) = S\bigl(\mathcal{A}_{pqr}, m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)\bigr) - \sum_{\substack{s \prec r \\ |s|^{2(j(PQ)+1)} \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ|r|^2)}} S(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s).
\end{equation}
Inserting \eqref{eq: lemma 14 buchstab r} into \eqref{eq: lemma 14 buchstab q} and summing over the pairs of $S_\Ge$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 split} gives \eqref{eq: identity 36}.

The sum over $(p, q)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36} is $\sum_{n \neq 0} a_n S(\mathcal{A}_n, z(PQ))$, where $a_n$ is the number of pairs $(p, q)$ in the range of $\sum_{p, q}$ with $pq = n$. Each $a_n$ is $0$ or $1$, since $q \prec p$. By \eqref{eq: lemma 14 pq bounds}, every $n$ with $a_n \neq 0$ satisfies \eqref{eq: lemma 14 criterion} with $y = z(PQ)$. Lemma~\ref{lem: level small}, applied to the terms with $|n|^2 < X^{\sigma}$, and Lemma~\ref{lem: level large}, applied to the terms with $|n|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}$, give the sum over $(p, q)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36} an asymptotic formula.

The sum over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 36} is the configuration with variables $p, q, r$, ranges $[P, 2P)$, $[\max(Q, z(PQ)), 2Q)$ and $[z(PQ), 2Q)$, the constraints $q \prec p$ and $r \prec q$, and the sieve condition of type (M) with the level $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms}. The level $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$ has the form \eqref{eq: type M level} required in type (M), with $Z_{\mathrm{M}} = (X^{\tau}/(PQ))^{1/(j(PQ)+1)}$, with $f_i = -1/(j(PQ)+1)$ for the index of $r$, and with $f_i = 0$ for the indices of $p$ and $q$. The requirement $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) \Ge \mu^4$ of Definition~\ref{def: configuration} is \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms lower}.

The pieces of hypothesis (a) and the window pieces of the sum over $(p, q, r)$ need a lower bound for $|pqr|^2$. Every prime $r$ of this sum has $|r|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$, so \eqref{eq: z function}, the bound $PQ \Ge X^{\sigma - (j(PQ)+1)\theta}$ of \eqref{eq: j interval} and the relation $\tau = \sigma + 2\theta$ of \eqref{eq: parameters} give
\begin{equation*}
|pqr|^2 \Ge PQ\, z(PQ) = \begin{cases} (PQ\, X^{\tau})^{1/2} \Ge (X^{\sigma - \theta} X^{\tau})^{1/2} = X^{\sigma + \theta/2} & \text{if } j(PQ) = 0, \\ (PQ)^{2/3} X^{\tau/3} \Ge (X^{\sigma - 2\theta})^{2/3} X^{\tau/3} = X^{\sigma - 2\theta/3} & \text{if } j(PQ) = 1. \end{cases}
\end{equation*}
Every product $pqr$ of the sum over $(p, q, r)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 three primes}
|pqr|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma - j(PQ)\theta}, \qquad j(|pqr|^2) \Le j(PQ) - 1, \qquad z(|pqr|^2) \Ge \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{|pqr|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)}.
\end{equation}
In \eqref{eq: lemma 14 three primes}, the bound for $|pqr|^2$ follows from the lower bounds $X^{\sigma + \theta/2}$ and $X^{\sigma - 2\theta/3}$ for $PQ\, z(PQ)$ in the cases $j(PQ) = 0$ and $j(PQ) = 1$. If $|pqr|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}$, then $j(|pqr|^2) = -1$ by \eqref{eq: j function}. If $|pqr|^2 < X^{\sigma}$, then \eqref{eq: j interval} and the bound for $|pqr|^2$ give
\begin{equation*}
X^{\sigma - j(PQ)\theta} \Le |pqr|^2 < X^{\sigma - j(|pqr|^2)\theta},
\end{equation*}
so $j(|pqr|^2) < j(PQ)$. Since $|pqr|^2 < X^{\tau}$ by \eqref{eq: lemma 14 small products} with $\varpi = r$, \eqref{eq: z function} and the bound for $j(|pqr|^2)$ give the bound for $z(|pqr|^2)$:
\begin{equation*}
z(|pqr|^2) = \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{|pqr|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(|pqr|^2)+2)} \Ge \Bigl( \frac{X^{\tau}}{|pqr|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)}.
\end{equation*}

By \eqref{eq: piece level}, a piece of hypothesis (a) of Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} has the level $y = \inf_{(p, q, r) \in \mathcal{T}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})} m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$. Every tuple $(p, q, r)$ of the piece has $|pqr|^2 < 4PQ|r|^2$, so \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms} and \eqref{eq: lemma 14 three primes} give
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 piece level}
y \Le m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) < \Bigl( \frac{4X^{\tau}}{|pqr|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)} \Le 4z(|pqr|^2).
\end{equation}
With the bound $|pqr|^2 \Le X^{\tau - 3\varepsilon}$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 14 small products} for $\varpi = r$, the piece satisfies \eqref{eq: lemma 14 criterion} with $n = pqr$ and falls under (P4).

The straddling pieces fall under (P1) by \eqref{eq: lemma 14 small products} with $\varpi = r$. A window piece adds a prime $\varpi'$. On the piece, $|r|^2$ varies by at most the factor $1 + 1/H$, by \eqref{eq: short intervals}. By \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms}, the level $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$ depends on $(p, q, r)$ only through the factor $|r|^{-2/(j(PQ)+1)}$, so $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$ also varies by at most the factor $1 + 1/H$ on the piece. By \eqref{eq: window} and the variation of $m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)$ on the piece, $|\varpi'|^2$ lies in a short interval $J_{\lambda'}$ with
\begin{equation*}
\xi_{\lambda'} < \sup_{(p, q, r) \in \mathcal{T}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})} m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) \Le (1 + 1/H)\, m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r)
\end{equation*}
for every tuple $(p, q, r)$ of the piece. Hence \eqref{eq: short intervals} and \eqref{eq: lemma 14 piece level} give, for every tuple $(p, q, r)$ of the piece,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lemma 14 window}
|\varpi'|^2 < (1 + 1/H)\, \xi_{\lambda'} < (1 + 1/H)^2\, m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) < (1 + 1/H)^2 \Bigl( \frac{4X^{\tau}}{|pqr|^2} \Bigr)^{1/(j(PQ)+1)}.
\end{equation}
If $j(PQ) = 0$, then \eqref{eq: lemma 14 window} and \eqref{eq: lemma 14 three primes} give
\begin{equation*}
|pqr\varpi'|^2 < 4(1 + 1/H)^2 X^{\tau} \Le 5X^{\tau}, \qquad |pqr|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}.
\end{equation*}
So the window piece falls under (P3) by \eqref{eq: P3 criterion}, with $\mathcal{S} = \{p, q, r\}$ and $\varpi_{i_0} = \varpi'$. If $j(PQ) = 1$, then \eqref{eq: lemma 14 window} and the bound $|pqr|^2 \Le X^{\tau - 3\varepsilon}$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 14 small products} with $\varpi = r$ give
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
|pqr\varpi'|^2 &< (1 + 1/H)^2\, |pqr|^2 \Bigl( \frac{4X^{\tau}}{|pqr|^2} \Bigr)^{1/2} = 2(1 + 1/H)^2 \bigl( X^{\tau} |pqr|^2 \bigr)^{1/2} \\
&\Le 2(1 + 1/H)^2 X^{\tau - 3\varepsilon/2} \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon},
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
so the window piece falls under (P1). Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} gives the sum over $(p, q, r)$ an asymptotic formula.
\end{proof}

\section{The ranges and the discarded regions}
\label{sec: decomposition}

This section proves identity \eqref{eq: identity 37}, defines the regions of Table~\ref{tab: ranges}, and checks the treatment of each range.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: identity 37}
Let $\Pi_1$ and $\Pi_2$ be as in \eqref{eq: Pi}, and $\sum^*$ as in \eqref{eq: identity 37}. Identity \eqref{eq: identity 37} holds for $\mathcal{E} \in \{\mathcal{A}, \mathcal{B}\}$. The sums $S(\mathcal{E}, X^{\theta})$, $\sum_{p \in \Pi_1} S(\mathcal{E}_p, z(|p|^2))$ and $\sum_{p \in \Pi_2} S(\mathcal{E}_p, p)$ of \eqref{eq: identity 37} have asymptotic formulas. A prime $p$ with $|p|^2 = X^{\alpha}$ lies in $\Pi_2$ exactly when $\theta \Le \alpha < \tau/5$ or $\sigma - 2\theta \Le \alpha < \tau/4$. The interval $[\sigma - 2\theta, \tau/4)$ is empty for $\theta \Le 3/26$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} for $S'(\mathcal{E}_1, X^{1/2})$, with $w = X^{\theta}$, gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: buchstab theta}
S(\mathcal{E}, X^{1/2}) = S(\mathcal{E}, X^{\theta}) - \sum_{X^{\theta} \Le |p|^2 < X^{1/2}} S(\mathcal{E}_p, p),
\end{equation}
and $S(\mathcal{E}_p, p) = S'(\mathcal{E}_p, p)$ by \eqref{eq: sifting} and \eqref{eq: cofactor sieve}. For $p \in \Pi_1$, the identity of \eqref{eq: buchstab} for $S'(\mathcal{E}_u, q)$, with $p$ in place of both $u$ and $q$ and with $w = z(|p|^2) \Le |p|^2$, gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: buchstab Pi1}
S'(\mathcal{E}_p, p) = S\bigl(\mathcal{E}_p, z(|p|^2)\bigr) - \sum_{\substack{q \prec p \\ |q|^2 \Ge z(|p|^2)}} S(\mathcal{E}_{pq}, q).
\end{equation}
By \eqref{eq: sifting} and \eqref{eq: cofactor sieve}, the functions $S(\mathcal{E}_p, z(|p|^2))$ and $S(\mathcal{E}_{pq}, q)$ in \eqref{eq: buchstab Pi1} equal the functions $S'(\mathcal{E}_p, z(|p|^2))$ and $S'(\mathcal{E}_{pq}, q)$ that \eqref{eq: buchstab} gives, since $z(|p|^2) \Le |p|^2$ by \eqref{eq: Pi} and $q \prec p$. Inserting \eqref{eq: buchstab Pi1} into \eqref{eq: buchstab theta} for $p \in \Pi_1$ gives \eqref{eq: identity 37}.

The term $S(\mathcal{E}, X^{\theta})$ of \eqref{eq: identity 37} has an asymptotic formula by Corollary~\ref{cor: level one}. In the sum of $S(\mathcal{E}_p, z(|p|^2)) = S'(\mathcal{E}_p, z(|p|^2))$ over $p \in \Pi_1$, for $|p|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma}$ the level $z(|p|^2)$ is $X^{\tau}/|p|^2$ by \eqref{eq: j function} and \eqref{eq: z function}, and $|p|^2 < X^{1/2} = X^{\tau - \theta}$, so Lemma~\ref{lem: level large} applies with $C_l = 1$. For $|p|^2 < X^{\sigma}$, Lemma~\ref{lem: level small} applies with $C_l = 1$ and the level $z(|p|^2)$.

By \eqref{eq: Pi} and \eqref{eq: beta0}, a prime $p$ with $|p|^2 = X^{\alpha}$ and $\theta \Le \alpha < 1/2$ lies in $\Pi_2$ exactly when $\beta_0(\alpha) > \alpha$. For $\alpha \Ge \sigma$, $\beta_0(\alpha) = \tau - \alpha \Le \alpha$, since $\sigma > \tau/2$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. For $\alpha < \sigma$, \eqref{eq: j interval} applied to $X^{\alpha}$ and \eqref{eq: beta0} give
\begin{equation*}
\sigma - (j(X^{\alpha}) + 1)\theta \Le \alpha < \sigma - j(X^{\alpha})\theta, \qquad \beta_0(\alpha) = \frac{\tau - \alpha}{j(X^{\alpha}) + 2} > \alpha \iff \alpha < \frac{\tau}{j(X^{\alpha}) + 3}.
\end{equation*}
For $j(X^{\alpha}) = 0$ this is impossible, because then $\alpha \Ge \sigma - \theta > \tau/3$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. For $j(X^{\alpha}) = 1$ it gives $\alpha \in [\sigma - 2\theta, \tau/4)$, since $\tau/4 < \tau/3 < \sigma - \theta$, and $\tau/4 \Le \sigma - 2\theta$ for $\theta \Le 3/26$. For $j(X^{\alpha}) = 2$ it gives $\theta \Le \alpha < \tau/5$, since $\theta > \sigma - 3\theta$ and $\tau/5 < \sigma - 2\theta$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. For $j(X^{\alpha}) \Ge 3$, $\alpha < \sigma - 3\theta < \theta$, which is excluded.

In the sum over $p \in \Pi_2$, \eqref{eq: sifting} and \eqref{eq: cofactor sieve} show that the term $S(\mathcal{E}_p, p)$ differs from $S'(\mathcal{E}_p, |p|^2)$ only by the cofactors divisible by the other prime $p'$ of norm $|p|^2$, when it exists and $p' \prec p$. So $0 \Le S'(\mathcal{E}_p, |p|^2) - S(\mathcal{E}_p, p) \Le |\mathcal{E}_{pp'}|$. For $p \in \Pi_2$, $|p|^2 < z(|p|^2)$ by \eqref{eq: Pi}, and $|p|^2 < X^{\tau/4} < X^{\sigma}$ by the description of $\Pi_2$, so Lemma~\ref{lem: level small} gives the sum of $S'(\mathcal{E}_p, |p|^2)$ over $p \in \Pi_2$ an asymptotic formula. Since $|pp'|^2 = |p|^4 < X^{\tau/2}$, Lemmas~\ref{lem: type I}(a) and~\ref{lem: counting}(a) give, with $p$ restricted to the primes of $\Pi_2$ for which $p'$ exists and $p' \prec p$,
\begin{equation*}
\sum_{p \in \Pi_2} |\mathcal{A}_{pp'}| = \frac{2}{\pi X^{\sigma}} \sum_{p \in \Pi_2} |\mathcal{B}_{pp'}| + \mathcal{O}\bigl(X^{\tau}(\log X)^{-A}\bigr), \qquad \sum_{p \in \Pi_2} |\mathcal{B}_{pp'}| \ll X \sum_{|p|^2 \Ge X^{\theta}} |p|^{-4} \ll X^{1 - \theta}.
\end{equation*}
So the sums of $|\mathcal{A}_{pp'}|$ and of $2|\mathcal{B}_{pp'}|/(\pi X^{\sigma})$ over $p \in \Pi_2$ are $\mathcal{O}(X^{\tau}(\log X)^{-A})$, and the sum of $S(\mathcal{E}_p, p)$ over $p \in \Pi_2$ has an asymptotic formula.
\end{proof}

At $\theta = 0.1129$ the primes of $\Pi_2$ have $\alpha \in [0.1129, 0.12258)$, so $\mathsf{D}$ begins at $\alpha = \tau/5 = 0.12258$ in Figure~\ref{fig: domain}.

In the sum $\sum^*$, every element counted by a term $S(\mathcal{E}_{pq}, q)$ with $|p|^2 |q|^4 \Ge X$ has cofactor $t = 1$, because a cofactor $t \neq 1$ has $|t|^2 \Ge |q|^2$ and would give $|pqt|^2 \Ge |p|^2 |q|^4 \Ge X$. Then $pq \in \mathcal{E}$, $q \prec p$ and $|p|^2 < X^{1/2}$ force $X^{1/2}/2 < |q|^2 \Le |p|^2 < X^{1/2}$, and Lemma~\ref{lem: prime ideal theorem}(d) bounds the number of such pairs with $pq \in \mathcal{B}$ by $\mathcal{O}(X/(\log X)^2)$. These terms are non-negative and enter \eqref{eq: identity 37} with the sign $+$, so we discard them. Their contribution tends to $0$ after division by $S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2})$, which is of order $X/\log X$ by \eqref{eq: primes in B}. The remaining pairs have exponents in the closure of the domain $\mathsf{D}$ of \eqref{eq: domain}.

On each part of Ranges~1 to~6, the hypotheses of the lemmas used there are linear inequalities in $\alpha$, $\beta$ and $\theta$. For Lemmas~\ref{lem: lemma 13} and~\ref{lem: lemma 14}, the hypotheses on $P$ and $Q$ read, in exponents and up to the margin $\varepsilon$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: exponent hypotheses}
\begin{aligned}
&\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13}:} && \varepsilon \Le \beta \Le \alpha, \quad \sigma \Le \alpha + \beta \Le \tau, \quad \text{and (a) or (b) of \eqref{eq: lemma 12 cases}:} \\
& && \text{(a)}\ \alpha + \beta \Le \zeta,\ \beta \Le \rho,\ \alpha + 2\beta \Le \nu; \qquad \text{(b)}\ \alpha \Le \zeta,\ 2\beta \Le \rho,\ \alpha + 2\beta \Le \nu; \\
&\text{Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14}:} && \varepsilon \Le \beta \Le \alpha, \quad \sigma - 2\theta \Le \alpha + \beta < \sigma.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
In Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13}, (a) or (b) is needed only for the sum over $(p, q, r)$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35}. On $\mathsf{D}$, $\beta < \alpha$ by \eqref{eq: domain}, and $\beta \Ge c(\theta) > \varepsilon$ with $c(\theta)$ of \eqref{eq: exponent floor}, so $\varepsilon \Le \beta \Le \alpha$ holds on every range. Where Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} is applied below, $\alpha + \beta \Le \tau$ holds: in case (a), since $\alpha + \beta \Le \zeta < \tau$; on $\mathsf{R}$, since $\alpha + \beta < \sigma + \rho/2 < \tau$; and where $\beta < (\tau - \alpha)/2$, since then $\alpha + \beta < (\alpha + \tau)/2 < \tau$. Appendix~\ref{app: inequalities} lists the inequalities between the parameters that these hypotheses use, with their values at $\theta = 0.1129$. On each range, the discarded region consists of the pairs on which no lemma of Section~\ref{sec: two primes} applies.

Range~1 consists of the pairs with $\sigma \Le \alpha < 1/2$. Here $\beta_0(\alpha) = \tau - \alpha$ by \eqref{eq: beta0}, and $\beta < (1 - \alpha)/2 < \alpha$, since $\alpha \Ge \sigma > 1/3$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. The hypotheses $X^{\sigma} \Le P \Le X^{\zeta - \varepsilon}$, $Q \Ge P X^{-2\theta}$ and $PQ \Le X^{\nu - \varepsilon}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 11} read $\sigma \Le \alpha \Le \zeta$, $\beta \Ge \alpha - 2\theta$ and $\alpha + \beta \Le \nu$ in exponents. So Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 11} gives the sum $\sum^*$ over a box an asymptotic formula where these inequalities hold. Lemmas~\ref{lem: lemma 13} and~\ref{lem: lemma 14} do not apply in Range~1, since $\beta \Ge \tau - \alpha$ gives $\alpha + \beta \Ge \tau$ there. The discarded region is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: D1}
\mathsf{D}_1 := \bigl\{ \sigma \Le \alpha < 1/2,\ \tau - \alpha \Le \beta < (1 - \alpha)/2 \bigr\} \setminus \bigl\{ \alpha - 2\theta \Le \beta \Le \nu - \alpha \bigr\}.
\end{equation}
The set subtracted in $\mathsf{D}_1$ is empty for $\alpha > (\nu + 2\theta)/2$, since $\alpha - 2\theta \Le \nu - \alpha$ means $\alpha \Le (\nu + 2\theta)/2$. Since $(\nu + 2\theta)/2 < \zeta$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, the condition $\alpha \Le \zeta$ of Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 11} holds wherever this set is non-empty.

Range~2 consists of the pairs with $1/3 \Le \alpha < \sigma$. Here $\beta_0(\alpha) = (\tau - \alpha)/2$, as Section~\ref{subsec: groupings} shows, and $\beta < (1 - \alpha)/2 \Le \alpha$, since $\alpha \Ge 1/3$. On the whole range, $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/2$ and $\alpha \Ge 1/3$ give $\alpha + \beta \Ge (\alpha + \tau)/2 \Ge (1/3 + \tau)/2 > \sigma$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, so $PQ \Ge X^{\sigma}$. The bound $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/2$ is $|q|^2 \Ge X^{\tau}/|pq|^2$ in exponents, so $S_< = 0$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 13 split} on every box at distance at least $\varepsilon$ from the boundary of $\mathsf{D}$. Where $\alpha + \beta \Le \zeta$, case (a) of \eqref{eq: exponent hypotheses} holds, since $\beta \Le \zeta - 1/3 < \rho$ and $\alpha + 2\beta \Le 2\zeta - 1/3 < \nu$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. On the region $\mathsf{R}$ of \eqref{eq: region R}, case (b) holds by Section~\ref{subsec: groupings}. The discarded region is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: D2}
\mathsf{D}_2 := \bigl\{ 1/3 \Le \alpha < \sigma,\ \max(\zeta - \alpha, (\tau - \alpha)/2) \Le \beta < (1 - \alpha)/2,\ \beta > \rho/2 \bigr\}.
\end{equation}

Range~3 consists of the pairs with $\rho \Le \alpha < 1/3$. Here \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $j(X^{\alpha}) = 0$, since $\rho > \sigma - \theta$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, so $\beta_0(\alpha) = (\tau - \alpha)/2$ by \eqref{eq: beta0}, and $\beta < \alpha$. Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} applies in case (a) of \eqref{eq: exponent hypotheses} where $\alpha + \beta \Le \zeta$. Indeed, there, by \eqref{eq: parameters} and Table~\ref{tab: inequalities},
\begin{equation*}
\beta \Le \zeta - \rho < \rho, \qquad \alpha + 2\beta \Le 2\zeta - \rho = \nu, \qquad \alpha + \beta \Ge \frac{\alpha + \tau}{2} \Ge \frac{\rho + \tau}{2} > \sigma.
\end{equation*} Where $\alpha + \beta > \zeta$, case (b) fails, since $2\beta > 2(\zeta - 1/3) = 8\theta/3 > \rho$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. The discarded region is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: D4}
\mathsf{D}_4 := \bigl\{ \rho \Le \alpha < 1/3,\ \zeta - \alpha \Le \beta < \alpha \bigr\}.
\end{equation}
Its lower limit $\zeta - \alpha$ of $\beta$ exceeds $\beta_0(\alpha) = (\tau - \alpha)/2$, since $\alpha < 1/3 < 2\zeta - \tau$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}.

Range~4 consists of the pairs with $\sigma - \theta \Le \alpha < \rho$. Here \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $j(X^{\alpha}) = 0$, since $\sigma - \theta \Le \alpha < \rho < \sigma$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, so $\beta_0(\alpha) = (\tau - \alpha)/2$ by \eqref{eq: beta0}. Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} applies in case (a) of \eqref{eq: exponent hypotheses} where $\alpha + 2\beta \Le \nu$. Indeed, there, by \eqref{eq: parameters},
\begin{equation*}
\alpha + \beta \Le \frac{\alpha + \nu}{2} < \frac{\rho + \nu}{2} = \zeta, \qquad \beta \Le \frac{\nu - \sigma + \theta}{2} = \frac{\rho + \theta}{2} < \rho,
\end{equation*}
and $\alpha + \beta \Ge (\alpha + \tau)/2 \Ge (1 - \theta)/2 > \sigma$. Where $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/2$ in Ranges~4 and~5, case (b) fails, since $\alpha < \rho$ gives $2\beta \Ge \tau - \alpha > \tau - \rho > \rho$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. The discarded region is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: D6}
\mathsf{D}_6 := \bigl\{ \sigma - \theta \Le \alpha < \rho,\ (\nu - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta < \alpha \bigr\}.
\end{equation}
Its lower limit $(\nu - \alpha)/2$ of $\beta$ exceeds $\beta_0(\alpha) = (\tau - \alpha)/2$, since $\tau < \nu$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}.

Range~5 consists of the pairs with $\alpha < \sigma - \theta$ and $\alpha + \beta \Ge \sigma - \theta$. Here \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $j(X^{\alpha}) \Ge 1$, so $\beta_0(\alpha) \Le (\tau - \alpha)/3$ by \eqref{eq: beta0}, and pairs with $\beta < (\tau - \alpha)/2$ occur. Where $\alpha + \beta \Ge \sigma$ and $\beta < (\tau - \alpha)/2$, the sum of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pq}, q)$ over a box is $S_<$ of \eqref{eq: lemma 13 split}, and Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} gives it an asymptotic formula. Where $\alpha + \beta \Ge \sigma$, $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/2$ and $\alpha + 2\beta \Le \nu$, Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} applies in case (a) of \eqref{eq: exponent hypotheses}, since there $\beta < \alpha < \sigma - \theta < \rho$ and $\alpha + \beta \Le (\alpha + \nu)/2 < (\sigma - \theta + \nu)/2 < \zeta$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. Where $\alpha + \beta < \sigma$, \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $j(X^{\alpha + \beta}) = 0$, and Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} applies with $j(PQ) = 0$. For $j(PQ) = 0$, the condition $|q|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$ of $S_\Ge$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 split} reads, by \eqref{eq: z function} and in exponents, $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha - \beta)/2$, that is, $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/3$. Since $\beta < \alpha$, the pairs of $S_\Ge$ have $\alpha > \tau/4$. The discarded region is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: D8}
\mathsf{D}_8 := \bigl\{ \nu/3 \Le \alpha < \sigma - \theta,\ (\nu - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta < \alpha \bigr\}.
\end{equation}
On $\mathsf{D}_8$, $\beta \Ge (\nu - \alpha)/2 > (\tau - \alpha)/2$, and $\alpha + \beta \Ge (\alpha + \nu)/2 \Ge 2\nu/3 > \sigma$, since $\alpha \Ge \nu/3$ and $\sigma < 2\nu/3$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. So the conditions $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/2$ and $\alpha + \beta \Ge \sigma$ of the part of Range~5 that $\mathsf{D}_8$ covers hold on all of $\mathsf{D}_8$.

Range~6 consists of the pairs with $\alpha + \beta < \sigma - \theta$. Since $p \in \Pi_1$, Lemma~\ref{lem: identity 37} gives $\alpha \Ge \tau/5 > \sigma - 3\theta$, by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. So \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $j(X^{\alpha}) \Le 2$, and \eqref{eq: beta0} gives $\beta \Ge \beta_0(\alpha) \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/4$. Hence
\begin{equation*}
\alpha + \beta \Ge \frac{3\alpha + \tau}{4} \Ge \frac{2\tau}{5} > \sigma - 2\theta,
\end{equation*}
by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, so \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $j(X^{\alpha + \beta}) = 1$, and Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} applies with $j(PQ) = 1$. For $j(PQ) = 1$, the condition $|q|^2 \Ge z(PQ)$ of $S_\Ge$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 split} reads, by \eqref{eq: z function} and in exponents, $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha - \beta)/3$, that is, $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/4$. So $S_< = 0$ in \eqref{eq: lemma 14 split} on every box at distance at least $\varepsilon$ from the boundary of $\mathsf{D}$. With $\alpha + \beta < \sigma - \theta$ and \eqref{eq: parameters}, the bound $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/4$ gives $\alpha < (4\sigma - 4\theta - \tau)/3 = \sigma - 2\theta$.

In the third sum of \eqref{eq: identity 35}, the exponents satisfy $\tau - \alpha - \beta \Le \delta < \gamma < \beta$. In that of \eqref{eq: identity 36}, with $j(X^{\alpha + \beta})$ in place of $j(PQ)$, they satisfy
\begin{equation*}
\frac{\tau - \alpha - \beta}{j(X^{\alpha + \beta}) + 2} \Le \gamma < \beta, \qquad \frac{\tau - \alpha - \beta - \gamma}{j(X^{\alpha + \beta}) + 1} \Le \delta < \gamma.
\end{equation*}
With these limits, the third sums are discarded over the following regions of $(\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \delta)$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: four dim regions}
\begin{aligned}
\mathsf{E}_3 &: 1/3 \Le \alpha < \sigma,\ (\tau - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta \Le \zeta - \alpha,\ \tau - \alpha - \beta \Le \delta < \gamma < \beta; \\
\mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{R}} &: (\alpha, \beta) \in \mathsf{R},\ \tau - \alpha - \beta \Le \delta < \gamma < \beta; \\
\mathsf{E}_5 &: \rho \Le \alpha < 1/3,\ (\tau - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta \Le \zeta - \alpha,\ \tau - \alpha - \beta \Le \delta < \gamma < \beta; \\
\mathsf{E}_7 &: \sigma - \theta \Le \alpha < \rho,\ (\tau - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta \Le (\nu - \alpha)/2,\ \tau - \alpha - \beta \Le \delta < \gamma < \beta; \\
\mathsf{E}_9 &: \alpha < \sigma - \theta,\ (\tau - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta \Le \min((\nu - \alpha)/2, \alpha),\ \tau - \alpha - \beta \Le \delta < \gamma < \beta; \\
\mathsf{E}_{10} &: \tau/4 \Le \alpha \Le \sigma - \theta,\ (\tau - \alpha)/3 \Le \beta < \min(\alpha, \sigma - \alpha), \\
& \qquad (\tau - \alpha - \beta)/2 \Le \gamma < \beta,\ \tau - \alpha - \beta - \gamma \Le \delta < \gamma; \\
\mathsf{E}_{11} &: \tau/5 \Le \alpha \Le \sigma - 2\theta,\ (\tau - \alpha)/4 \Le \beta < \min(\alpha, \sigma - \theta - \alpha), \\
& \qquad (\tau - \alpha - \beta)/3 \Le \gamma < \beta,\ (\tau - \alpha - \beta - \gamma)/2 \Le \delta < \gamma.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
In $\mathsf{E}_9$, $(\tau - \alpha)/2 \Le \beta \Le \alpha$ gives $\alpha \Ge \tau/3$, so $\alpha + \beta \Ge (\alpha + \tau)/2 \Ge 2\tau/3 > \sigma$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, and the pairs of $\mathsf{E}_9$ lie in the part of Range~5 where Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} applies. In $\mathsf{E}_{10}$, $\alpha \Ge \tau/4$ and $\beta \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/3$ give $\alpha + \beta \Ge (2\alpha + \tau)/3 \Ge \tau/2 > \sigma - \theta$, so the pairs of $\mathsf{E}_{10}$ lie in Range~5. The region $\mathsf{E}_{11}$ contains the set of exponents over which the third sum is discarded in Range~6, since $\beta_0(\alpha) \Ge (\tau - \alpha)/4$. For $\theta < 3/26$, the primes with $\alpha \in [\tau/4, \sigma - 2\theta)$ have $j(X^{\alpha}) = 2$ by \eqref{eq: j interval}, since $\sigma - 3\theta < \tau/5 < \tau/4$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, and $\beta_0(\alpha) = (\tau - \alpha)/4$ by \eqref{eq: beta0}. Their pairs have $\alpha + \beta < 2\alpha < 2(\sigma - 2\theta) < \sigma$ by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}, so Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} treats them, with $j(PQ) = 0$ in Range~5 and $j(PQ) = 1$ in Range~6. Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} does not depend on $j(X^{\alpha})$, so the discarded third sums of these pairs lie in $\mathsf{E}_{10}$ and $\mathsf{E}_{11}$.

Lemma~\ref{lem: lower bound} needs a positive lower bound for the exponents and an upper bound for their sum. All exponents on $\mathsf{D}$ and on the regions \eqref{eq: four dim regions} are at least
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: exponent floor}
c(\theta) := \min\Bigl( 4\theta^2,\ \frac{5\theta}{2} - \frac{1}{4},\ \frac{26\theta - 1}{24},\ \frac{1 - 2\theta}{6} \Bigr) > 0,
\end{equation}
and their sums satisfy
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: exponent sum}
\alpha + \beta < 1 - c(\theta) \quad \text{on } \mathsf{D}, \qquad \alpha + \beta + \gamma + \delta < 1 - c(\theta) \quad \text{on the regions \eqref{eq: four dim regions}}.
\end{equation}
The minimum in \eqref{eq: exponent floor} is positive, since $4\theta^2 > 0$ and $5\theta/2 - 1/4$, $(26\theta - 1)/24$ and $(1 - 2\theta)/6$ are quantities of Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. On $\mathsf{D}$, $\alpha \Ge \theta$ and $\beta \Ge \beta_0(\alpha)$. For $\alpha \Ge \sigma$, \eqref{eq: beta0} gives $\beta_0(\alpha) = \tau - \alpha > \theta$. For $\alpha < \sigma$, \eqref{eq: j interval} gives $\alpha < \sigma - j(X^{\alpha})\theta$. Then $\alpha \Ge \theta$ gives $(j(X^{\alpha}) + 1)\theta < \sigma$, that is, $j(X^{\alpha}) + 2 < (\sigma + \theta)/\theta = 1/(2\theta)$, and $\tau - \alpha > \tau - \sigma = 2\theta$, so \eqref{eq: beta0} gives
\begin{equation*}
\beta_0(\alpha) = \frac{\tau - \alpha}{j(X^{\alpha}) + 2} > \frac{2\theta}{j(X^{\alpha}) + 2} > 4\theta^2.
\end{equation*}
Also $\alpha + \beta < 1 - \beta \Le 1 - c(\theta)$ on $\mathsf{D}$, since $\beta < (1 - \alpha)/2$ and $\beta \Ge c(\theta)$.

On the regions \eqref{eq: four dim regions}, $\delta < \gamma < \beta$, and $\beta \Le \alpha$: on $\mathsf{E}_9$, $\mathsf{E}_{10}$ and $\mathsf{E}_{11}$ by their definitions, and on the other regions by Table~\ref{tab: inequalities}. On $\mathsf{E}_3$ and $\mathsf{E}_5$, $\alpha \Ge \rho$, since $\rho < 1/3$, and $\zeta - \alpha \Le \alpha$ for $\alpha \Ge \rho$ by $\zeta < 2\rho$. On $\mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{R}}$, $\beta \Le \rho/2 < 1/3 \Le \alpha$. On $\mathsf{E}_7$, $(\nu - \alpha)/2 \Le \alpha$ for $\alpha \Ge \sigma - \theta$ by $\nu/3 < \sigma - \theta$. So it suffices to bound $\delta$ from below and $\alpha + 3\beta$ from above. On $\mathsf{E}_3$, $\mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{R}}$, $\mathsf{E}_5$, $\mathsf{E}_7$ and $\mathsf{E}_9$, the lower limit of $\delta$ is $\tau - \alpha - \beta$, so an upper bound for $\alpha + \beta$ bounds $\delta$ from below. On $\mathsf{E}_3$, $\mathsf{E}_5$, $\mathsf{E}_7$ and $\mathsf{E}_9$, the pairs lie in the parts of Ranges~2 to~5 where case (a) of \eqref{eq: exponent hypotheses} holds, so $\alpha + \beta \Le \zeta$. On $\mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{R}}$, $\alpha + \beta < \sigma + \rho/2$ by \eqref{eq: region R}. Hence, on these regions,
\begin{equation*}
\delta \Ge \tau - \alpha - \beta \Ge \min\Bigl( \tau - \zeta,\ \tau - \sigma - \frac{\rho}{2} \Bigr) = \min\Bigl( \frac{1 - 2\theta}{6},\ \frac{26\theta - 1}{24} \Bigr).
\end{equation*}
On $\mathsf{E}_{10}$, $\alpha + \beta < \sigma$ and $\gamma < \beta < \sigma/2$, so $\delta \Ge \tau - \alpha - \beta - \gamma > \tau - 3\sigma/2 = 5\theta/2 - 1/4$. On $\mathsf{E}_{11}$, $\alpha + \beta < \sigma - \theta$ and $\gamma < \beta < (\sigma - \theta)/2$, so
\begin{equation*}
\delta \Ge \frac{\tau - \alpha - \beta - \gamma}{2} > \frac{\tau - 3(\sigma - \theta)/2}{2} = 2\theta - \frac{1}{8} \Ge \frac{5\theta}{2} - \frac{1}{4}.
\end{equation*}

For the sums of the exponents in \eqref{eq: exponent sum}, $\alpha + \beta + \gamma + \delta \Le \alpha + 3\beta$, and
\begin{equation*}
\alpha + 3\beta \Le \begin{cases} 3\zeta - 2/3 & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_3, \\ \sigma + 3\rho/2 & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_{\mathsf{R}}, \\ 3\zeta - 2\rho & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_5, \\ (3\nu - \sigma + \theta)/2 & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_7, \\ 4\nu/3 & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_9, \\ 2\sigma & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_{10}, \\ 2\sigma - 2\theta & \text{on } \mathsf{E}_{11}. \end{cases}
\end{equation*}
On $\mathsf{E}_9$, the bound $4\nu/3$ follows from $\alpha + 2\beta \Le \nu$ and $\beta \Le \min((\nu - \alpha)/2, \alpha) \Le \nu/3$. By \eqref{eq: parameters}, the bounds for $\alpha + 3\beta$ are $1/3 + 4\theta$, $5/8 + 7\theta/4$, $5/6 + \theta/3$, $(5 + 18\theta)/8$, $(7 + 10\theta)/9$, $1 - 2\theta$ and $1 - 4\theta$, so each is below $11/12$ for $1/9 < \theta < 1/8$. Also $11/12 < 1 - c(\theta)$, since $c(\theta) \Le 4\theta^2 < 1/16$.

\section{\texorpdfstring{Asymptotic formulas on the regions $\mathsf{G}$ and $\mathsf{L}$}{Asymptotic formulas on the regions G and L}}
\label{sec: four}

This section proves that the third sums of \eqref{eq: identity 35} and \eqref{eq: identity 36} have asymptotic formulas on the regions $\mathsf{G}$ and $\mathsf{L}$ of \eqref{eq: regions G L}. Let $p \sim P$, $q \sim Q$ be a box on which Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 13} or Lemma~\ref{lem: lemma 14} gives identity \eqref{eq: identity 35} or \eqref{eq: identity 36}. For powers $R$ and $R'$ of $2$, the quadruples $(p, q, r, s)$ of the third sum of that identity with $r \sim R$ and $s \sim R'$ form a \emph{four-dimensional box}. Its \emph{exponent box} is the set of the $(\alpha, \beta, \gamma, \delta)$ with $X^{\alpha} \in [P, 2P)$, $X^{\beta} \in [Q, 2Q)$, $X^{\gamma} \in [R, 2R)$ and $X^{\delta} \in [R', 2R')$. Let $\mathsf{G}_\varepsilon$ and $\mathsf{L}_\varepsilon$ be the regions defined by the inequalities of \eqref{eq: regions G L} with margin $\varepsilon$.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: four dim}
If the exponent box of a four-dimensional box lies in $\mathsf{G}_\varepsilon$ or in $\mathsf{L}_\varepsilon$, then the sum of $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s)$ over the box has an asymptotic formula.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
All ranges lie in $[\mu^4, X]$, as Definition~\ref{def: configuration} requires. Indeed, all norms are below $2P < X$, and $|p|^2 \Ge |q|^2 \Ge Q \Ge X^{\varepsilon}$ by the hypotheses of Lemmas~\ref{lem: lemma 13} and~\ref{lem: lemma 14}. In \eqref{eq: identity 35}, $|r|^2$ and $|s|^2$ are at least $X^{\tau}/(PQ) \Ge X^{\varepsilon}$, since $PQ \Le X^{\tau - \varepsilon}$. In \eqref{eq: identity 36}, $|r|^2 \Ge z(PQ) > X^{\theta}$ by \eqref{eq: z lower}, and $|s|^2 \Ge m_{\mathrm{M}}(p, q, r) > X^{2\varepsilon}$ by \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms} and \eqref{eq: lemma 14 ms lower}. Since $s \prec r \prec q \prec p$, \eqref{eq: sifting} and \eqref{eq: cofactor sieve} give $S(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s) = S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s)$. The sum of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s)$ over the box is the configuration with variables $p, q, r, s$ and the box ranges. Its constraints are $q \prec p$, $r \prec q$ and $s \prec r$, and the lower limits of $|q|^2$, $|r|^2$ and $|s|^2$ in \eqref{eq: identity 35} or \eqref{eq: identity 36}. Its sieve condition is of type (S), with $i_{\mathrm{S}}$ the index of $s$. The lower limits of $|q|^2$ and $|r|^2$ are constants, and so is that of $|s|^2$ for \eqref{eq: identity 35}. For \eqref{eq: identity 36}, the lower limit of $|s|^2$ is the monomial constraint $|r|^2|s|^{2(j(PQ)+1)} \Ge X^{\tau}/(PQ)$, with $j(PQ)$ of \eqref{eq: j function}. Its exponent $j(PQ) + 1$ is at most $2$, as Definition~\ref{def: configuration} requires, since $j(PQ) \in \{0, 1\}$. By Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling}, it suffices to check the pieces.

Suppose the exponent box lies in $\mathsf{G}_\varepsilon$. At one point of the exponent box, a non-empty set $\mathcal{S} \subset \{p, q, r\}$ satisfies $\sigma \Le x_{\mathcal{S}} \Le \tau - \delta$ with margin $\varepsilon$. The exponent box has diameter $\mathcal{O}(1/\log X)$, so $\mathcal{S}$ satisfies these inequalities with margin $\varepsilon/2$ on the whole exponent box. Every piece of hypotheses (a), (b) and (c) of Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} falls under (P3) of Lemma~\ref{lem: pieces} with this $\mathcal{S}$ and $\varpi_{i_0} = s$. Indeed, $x_{\mathcal{S}} \Ge \sigma + \varepsilon/2$ and $x_{\mathcal{S}} + \delta \Le \tau - \varepsilon/2$ on the exponent box, and $1 - \tau = \sigma$ by \eqref{eq: parameters}, so
\begin{equation*}
\Bigl| \prod_{i \in \mathcal{S}} \varpi_i \Bigr|^2 \Ge X^{\sigma + \varepsilon/2} \Ge X^{\sigma}, \qquad \frac{X/2}{\sup|s \prod_{i \in \mathcal{S}} \varpi_i|^2} \Ge \frac{X^{1 - \tau + \varepsilon/2}}{2} \Ge X^{\sigma}.
\end{equation*}

Suppose the exponent box lies in $\mathsf{L}_\varepsilon$. Since $|s|^2 \Ge \mu^4$, Buchstab's identity \eqref{eq: buchstab} at the level $\mu^4$, with $s'$ running over primes, gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: L split}
S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s) = S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, \mu^4) - \sum_{s' \prec s,\ |s'|^2 \Ge \mu^4} S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrss'}, s').
\end{equation}
Summed over the box, the term $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, \mu^4)$ of \eqref{eq: L split} is the configuration with the same variables and constraints, and with the sieve condition of type (C). As for $\mathsf{G}$, one of the alternatives that define $\mathsf{I}$ in \eqref{eq: regions G L} holds with margin $\varepsilon/2$ on the whole exponent box, so the pieces of hypothesis (a) fall under (P2), and the straddling pieces under (P1), with $\varepsilon/4$ in place of $\varepsilon$. There are no window pieces, since the window \eqref{eq: window} is empty in type (C). The sum of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrss'}, s')$ in \eqref{eq: L split}, over the box, is the configuration with the further prime variable $s'$ of range $[\mu^4, 2R')$, the further constraint $s' \prec s$, and the sieve condition of type (S) with $i_{\mathrm{S}}$ the index of $s'$. In the same way, a non-empty set $\mathcal{S} \subset \{p, q, r, s\}$ satisfies $\sigma \Le x_{\mathcal{S}} \Le \tau - \delta$ with margin $\varepsilon/2$ on the whole exponent box. On every tuple of a piece, $|s'|^2 < 2R' \Le 2|s|^2$, since $s'$ has range $[\mu^4, 2R')$ and $s \sim R'$. So the computation for $\mathsf{G}$ gives
\begin{equation*}
\frac{X/2}{\sup|s' \prod_{i \in \mathcal{S}} \varpi_i|^2} \Ge \frac{X/4}{\sup|s \prod_{i \in \mathcal{S}} \varpi_i|^2} \Ge \frac{X^{1 - \tau + \varepsilon/2}}{4} \Ge X^{\sigma}.
\end{equation*}
So every piece, the window pieces included, falls under (P3) with this $\mathcal{S}$ and $\varpi_{i_0} = s'$. Lemma~\ref{lem: decoupling} gives the sums of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, \mu^4)$ and of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrss'}, s')$ in \eqref{eq: L split} over the box asymptotic formulas. By \eqref{eq: L split}, the sum of $S'(\mathcal{A}_{pqrs}, s)$ over the box then has an asymptotic formula. In both cases, the asymptotic formula holds uniformly in $P$, $Q$, $R$ and $R'$ by the convention of Section~\ref{sec: notation} on implied constants.
\end{proof}

\section{The discarded sums and the lower bound}
\label{sec: integrals}

This section proves Lemma~\ref{lem: lower bound}, the lower bound announced in Section~\ref{sec: outline}. An element of $\mathcal{B}$ has norm in $[X/2, X)$, so it has no prime factor of norm below $X^{1/2}$ exactly when it is a Gaussian prime. So Lemma~\ref{lem: prime ideal theorem}(d), with the counting function $\pi_G$ of that lemma, gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: primes in B}
S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}) = \pi_G(X) - \pi_G(X/2) = (1 + o(1)) \frac{X}{2\log X}.
\end{equation}
The next lemma evaluates the sums over $\mathcal{B}$ with exponents in a given region, which bound the discarded sums.

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: boxes to integrals}
Let $K \Ge 1$ and $c_1 > 0$, and let $\mathcal{R} \subset \mathbb{R}^K$ be a bounded set whose boundary has Lebesgue measure $0$, and whose points $\boldsymbol{y} = (y_1, \dots, y_K)$ satisfy $y_i \Ge c_1$ and $y_K \Le y_i$ for all $i$, and $\sum_{i=1}^{K} y_i \Le 1 - c_1$.
With $\boldsymbol{y}(\boldsymbol{\varpi}) := (\log|\varpi_i|^2/\log X)_{i \Le K}$ for a tuple of primes $\boldsymbol{\varpi} = (\varpi_1, \dots, \varpi_K)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: boxes to integrals}
\sum_{\substack{\boldsymbol{y}(\boldsymbol{\varpi}) \in \mathcal{R} \\ \varpi_K \prec \varpi_i\ (i < K)}} S(\mathcal{B}_{\varpi_1 \cdots \varpi_K}, \varpi_K) = S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}) \Bigl( \int_{\mathcal{R}} \omega\Bigl( \frac{1 - \sum_{i=1}^{K} y_i}{y_K} \Bigr) \frac{d\boldsymbol{y}}{y_1 \cdots y_{K-1} y_K^2} + o(1) \Bigr).
\end{equation}
\emph{(Proof in Appendix~\ref{app: integrals}.)}
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}
\label{lem: lower bound}
For every small $\varepsilon > 0$ there is a number $\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta)$, with $\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta) \to \Phi(\theta)$ as $\varepsilon \to 0$, such that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lower bound}
S(\mathcal{A}, X^{1/2}) \Ge \frac{2}{\pi X^{\sigma}}\, S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}) \bigl( 1 - \Phi_\varepsilon(\theta) - o(1) \bigr).
\end{equation}
The same holds with $\Phi_{\mathsf{P}}$ in place of $\Phi$. In particular, if $\Phi(\theta) < 1$ or $\Phi_{\mathsf{P}}(\theta) < 1$, then $S(\mathcal{A}, X^{1/2}) \gg X^{\tau}/\log X$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Identity \eqref{eq: identity 37} holds for $\mathcal{A}$ and for $\mathcal{B}$, and its terms other than $\sum^*$ have asymptotic formulas by Lemma~\ref{lem: identity 37}. The domain $\mathsf{D}$ of \eqref{eq: domain} is divided by Ranges~1 to~6 into finitely many parts, which are polytopes, and Section~\ref{sec: decomposition} gives the treatment of each part. The hypotheses of the lemma applied on a part are finitely many linear inequalities in $(\alpha, \beta)$ that hold on the closed part (Section~\ref{sec: decomposition} and Appendix~\ref{app: inequalities}). Each of them has integer coefficients, not all $0$, so it holds with margin $\varepsilon$ at the points of the part at distance at least $\varepsilon$ from its boundary.

Split $\sum^*$ into the $\mathcal{O}((\log X)^2)$ dyadic boxes $p \sim P$, $q \sim Q$, whose exponent rectangles have side $\log 2/\log X$. Call a box \emph{good} if its exponent rectangle lies inside a part whose treatment is not a discard, at distance at least $\varepsilon$ from the boundary of that part. Then all its pairs belong to $\sum^*$, and the lemma of that part applies with margin. All other boxes are discarded, and so is the third sum of every good box on which \eqref{eq: identity 35} or \eqref{eq: identity 36} is used. Each discard leaves a lower bound for $S(\mathcal{A}, X^{1/2})$, since every term of $\sum^*$ is non-negative and enters \eqref{eq: identity 37} with the sign $+$, and every term of a third sum is non-negative and enters \eqref{eq: identity 35} or \eqref{eq: identity 36} with the sign $+$. For a discarded box, let $\Delta_{P,Q}(\mathcal{B})$ be the sum of $S(\mathcal{B}_{pq}, q)$ over the pairs of $\sum^*$ in the box. For a good box on which \eqref{eq: identity 35} or \eqref{eq: identity 36} is used, let $\Delta_{P,Q}(\mathcal{B})$ be the third sum of that identity for $\mathcal{E} = \mathcal{B}$, and for the other good boxes let $\Delta_{P,Q}(\mathcal{B}) := 0$. Summing the error terms of \eqref{eq: asymptotic formula} over the $\mathcal{O}((\log X)^2)$ good boxes, each $\mathcal{O}(X^{\tau}(\log X)^{-A})$ uniformly in the boxes by Section~\ref{sec: two primes}, and using \eqref{eq: identity 37} for $\mathcal{B}$, we obtain
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: lower bound boxes}
S(\mathcal{A}, X^{1/2}) \Ge \frac{2}{\pi X^{\sigma}} \Bigl( S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}) - \sum_{P, Q} \Delta_{P,Q}(\mathcal{B}) \Bigr) - \mathcal{O}\bigl( X^{\tau} (\log X)^{2 - A} \bigr),
\end{equation}
where $\sum_{P, Q}$ runs over the dyadic boxes $p \sim P$, $q \sim Q$ of $\sum^*$. In the third sums, split $r$ and $s$ into dyadic ranges, which gives the four-dimensional boxes of Section~\ref{sec: four}. Call a four-dimensional box \emph{good} if its exponent box lies in $\mathsf{G}_\varepsilon$ or in $\mathsf{L}_\varepsilon$. Lemma~\ref{lem: four dim} gives the sum over every good four-dimensional box an asymptotic formula, uniformly in the boxes. Every other four-dimensional box stays discarded. The number of four-dimensional boxes is $\mathcal{O}((\log X)^4)$. So \eqref{eq: lower bound boxes} holds with the sums over $\mathcal{B}$ of the good four-dimensional boxes removed from $\Delta_{P,Q}(\mathcal{B})$, and with $(\log X)^{4 - A}$ in place of $(\log X)^{2 - A}$.

The pairs of $\sum^*$ with $|p|^2|q|^4 \Ge X$ contribute $\mathcal{O}(X/(\log X)^2)$ to the discarded sums over $\mathcal{B}$ that remain in \eqref{eq: lower bound boxes} (Section~\ref{sec: decomposition}), and this is $o(S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}))$ by \eqref{eq: primes in B}. Every other pair of a discarded box has exponents in the closure of $\mathsf{D}$, within distance $2\varepsilon$ of a part whose treatment is a discard, of a boundary between parts, or of $\partial\mathsf{D}$, since the box is not good and its exponent rectangle has diameter $\mathcal{O}(1/\log X)$. The third sums use the quantities $X^{\tau}/(PQ)$ and $z(PQ)$, which are constant on a box, in place of $X^{\tau}/|pq|^2$ and $z(|pq|^2)$. Since $PQ \Le |pq|^2$, this raises the lower limits of $\gamma$ and $\delta$ in \eqref{eq: four dim regions}, so the exponents of every quadruple of a third sum of a good box lie in the closure of one of the regions \eqref{eq: four dim regions}. If the exponents of such a quadruple lie in $\mathsf{G}$ and its four-dimensional box is not good, then a point of the exponent box, at distance $\mathcal{O}(1/\log X)$ from them, lies outside $\mathsf{G}_\varepsilon$. The same holds for $\mathsf{L}$. So the exponents of every quadruple of a discarded four-dimensional box lie within distance $2\varepsilon$ of the points of the regions \eqref{eq: four dim regions} outside $\mathsf{G} \cup \mathsf{L}$, or of the boundary of one of the polytopes whose unions define $\mathsf{G}$, $\mathsf{I}$ and $\mathsf{L}$ in \eqref{eq: regions G L}.

Let $\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta)$ be the sum of the losses \eqref{eq: loss} of the following sets. One is the set of the points of the closure of $\mathsf{D}$ within distance $2\varepsilon$ of a part whose treatment is a discard, of a boundary between parts, or of $\partial\mathsf{D}$. For each region $\mathsf{E}_k$ of \eqref{eq: four dim regions}, another is the set of the points of the closure of $\mathsf{E}_k$ within distance $2\varepsilon$ of $\mathsf{E}_k \setminus (\mathsf{G} \cup \mathsf{L})$ or of the boundary of one of the polytopes whose unions define $\mathsf{G}$, $\mathsf{I}$ and $\mathsf{L}$. Lemma~\ref{lem: boxes to integrals}, with $K = 2$ and $K = 4$, bounds the other discarded sums over $\mathcal{B}$ by $S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2})(\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta) + o(1))$. Its hypotheses hold for these sets with $c_1 = c(\theta)$. The sets are bounded, and their boundaries have measure $0$, since each is the intersection of the closure of a finite union of polytopes with the set of the points within distance $2\varepsilon$ of a finite union of convex polytopes. On them, $y_i \Ge c_1$ and $\sum_{i=1}^{K} y_i \Le 1 - c_1$ by \eqref{eq: exponent floor} and \eqref{eq: exponent sum}, and $y_K \Le y_i$, since $\beta \Le \alpha$ on the closure of $\mathsf{D}$ and $\delta \Le \gamma \Le \beta \Le \alpha$ on the closures of the regions \eqref{eq: four dim regions}.

As $\varepsilon \to 0$, these sets decrease to the closures of the regions in \eqref{eq: total loss}, together with finitely many boundaries. The parts of $\mathsf{D}$ and the sets $\mathsf{G}$, $\mathsf{I}$, $\mathsf{L}$ and $\mathsf{P}$ are finite unions of polytopes, so these boundaries are null sets. The integrands are bounded, so $\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta) \to \Phi(\theta)$. Finally, $X^{\tau}(\log X)^{4 - A} = o(X^{-\sigma} S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}))$ for $A > 5$, by \eqref{eq: primes in B}. So, in \eqref{eq: lower bound boxes} with the good four-dimensional boxes removed and with $(\log X)^{4 - A}$ in place of $(\log X)^{2 - A}$, the discarded sums over $\mathcal{B}$ are at most $S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2})(\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta) + o(1))$ and the error term is $o(X^{-\sigma} S(\mathcal{B}, X^{1/2}))$, which gives \eqref{eq: lower bound}. The same argument applies with $\mathsf{P}$ in place of $\mathsf{L}$, since the inequalities of \eqref{eq: polytope} with margin $\varepsilon$ imply those of $\mathsf{L}$ with margin $\varepsilon$, by the computation after \eqref{eq: polytope}. If $\Phi(\theta) < 1$, fix $\varepsilon$ with $\Phi_\varepsilon(\theta) < 1$. Then \eqref{eq: lower bound} and \eqref{eq: primes in B} give $S(\mathcal{A}, X^{1/2}) \gg X^{\tau}/\log X$. The same holds if $\Phi_{\mathsf{P}}(\theta) < 1$.
\end{proof}

\section{Evaluation of the losses and proof of \texorpdfstring{Theorem~\ref{thm: sector}}{Theorem 2}}
\label{sec: numerics}

The losses \eqref{eq: loss} are computed numerically by two methods, which differ in the quadrature and in the way they remove $\mathsf{G} \cup \mathsf{L}$ from the regions $\mathsf{E}_k$, so that each checks the other. Both are implemented in the folder \texttt{Code} (see the data availability statement) and use the same limits of the regions and the same table of Buchstab's function $\omega$ of \eqref{eq: buchstab function}. The first method also uses a table of its integral $\Omega(u) := \int_1^u \omega(t)\, dt$. On each interval $[h, h + 1]$, $\omega$ is a Chebyshev series of degree $40$ and $\Omega$ its integral, of degree $41$, built for $h = 1, 2, \dots, 39$ in turn from \eqref{eq: buchstab function}, which determines $\omega$ on $[h, h + 1]$ from its values on $[1, h]$. They are tabulated on a grid of step $10^{-5}$ and evaluated between grid points by cubic Hermite interpolation, with the derivative $\omega$ of $\Omega$ and the derivative $\omega'(u) = (\omega(u - 1) - \omega(u))/u$ of $\omega$, which \eqref{eq: buchstab function} gives on each interval $[h, h + 1]$. By \eqref{eq: exponent floor}, every argument of $\omega$ in the losses is below $1/c(\theta)$, which is less than $40$, since each quantity in \eqref{eq: exponent floor} exceeds $1/36$ for $1/9 < \theta < 1/8$. On $[1, 3]$, $\omega$ has the closed forms
\begin{equation*}
\omega(u) = \frac{1}{u} \quad (1 \Le u \Le 2), \qquad \omega(u) = \frac{1 + \log(u - 1)}{u} \quad (2 \Le u \Le 3),
\end{equation*}
and the tables agree with these formulas and their integrals to $10^{-14}$. On $[2, 40)$, they satisfy \eqref{eq: buchstab function} to $1.5 \times 10^{-13}$.

In the first method, the integral over $\beta$ in the two-dimensional losses \eqref{eq: loss} is evaluated exactly through $\Omega$, since
\begin{equation}
\label{eq: Omega derivative}
\omega\Bigl( \frac{1 - \alpha - \beta}{\beta} \Bigr) \frac{1}{\beta^2} = -\frac{1}{1 - \alpha}\, \frac{d}{d\beta}\, \Omega\Bigl( \frac{1 - \alpha}{\beta} - 1 \Bigr).
\end{equation}
With $\alpha + \beta + \gamma$ in place of $\alpha$ and $\delta$ in place of $\beta$, \eqref{eq: Omega derivative} gives the integral over $\delta$ in the four-dimensional losses. For fixed $(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$, the part of the $\delta$-range outside $\mathsf{G} \cup \mathsf{L}$ is a finite union of intervals, and \eqref{eq: Omega derivative} is applied on each of them. The remaining integrals are computed by composite Gauss--Legendre quadrature. For the regions $\mathsf{D}_k$, the $\alpha$-axis is cut into $256$ equal panels. For the regions $\mathsf{E}_k$, each of the $\alpha$-, $\beta$- and $\gamma$-axes is cut into $16$ equal panels. The panels are cut further at the points where a limit of integration changes its formula, and at the points where a sum of some of $\alpha$, $\beta$ and $\gamma$ equals $\sigma$, $\rho$ or $\zeta$, at which the part of the $\delta$-range in $\mathsf{G} \cup \mathsf{L}$ jumps. On the $\gamma$-axis, they are also cut where the $\delta$-range becomes non-empty and where a limit of $\delta$ meets $(1 - \alpha - \beta - \gamma)/2$, the value at which the argument of $\omega$ equals $1$, since the integrand in $\gamma$ has a kink at these points. Each resulting subinterval carries $10$ Gauss--Legendre nodes.

In the second method, the two-dimensional losses are computed by nested adaptive Gauss--Kronrod quadrature of the integrand itself \citep{piessens1983quadpack}, and their error estimate is that of the outer adaptive rule. The four-dimensional losses are computed by scrambled Sobol quasi-Monte Carlo integration \citep{sobol1967distribution, matousek1998l2}, with $\omega$ read from its table instead of through $\Omega$ and with the sets $\mathsf{G}$ and $\mathsf{L}$ tested pointwise. Each of these losses is the mean of $8$ estimates, from independent scramblings of $2^{20}$ points each. The error estimate, which measures the spread of the $8$ estimates, is the square root of the sum of the squared deviations of the $8$ estimates from their mean, divided by $(8 \cdot 7)^{1/2}$.

Table~\ref{tab: losses} lists the losses at $\theta = 0.1129$. The first method gives $\Phi(0.1129) = 0.9990033$, and the second gives $0.9990044$. At $\theta = 0.119$, the decomposition of Harman and Lewis, with the regions derived from the lemmas (Remark~\ref{rem: misprints}) and with the cut by which Harman and Lewis exclude a subset of $\mathsf{G}$ from the region of $\varphi_7$ (Appendix~\ref{app: comparison}), gives the total $0.9425585$, below the bound $0.943$ of \citet[p.~134]{harman2001gaussian}. Table~\ref{tab: hl 0119} compares the losses with the bounds of Harman and Lewis region by region. Table~\ref{tab: roots} lists the values of $\theta$ at which the total loss equals $1$ as the groupings are added. Figure~\ref{fig: total loss} shows, as a function of $\theta$, the total loss of the decomposition of each row of Table~\ref{tab: roots}. At each of the $61$ values of $\theta$ in the figure, the total loss decreases down the rows of Table~\ref{tab: roots}.
